What to know There have been outbreaks of clade I monkeypox in Central and Eastern Africa since late 2023. Since January 2024, nearly 60,000 confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported globally, including more than 150 deaths. Newer outbreaks in several Western European countries are likely related to this ongoing outbreak. Transmission dynamics are changing. Only a small proportion of exported clade I monkeypox cases worldwide this year have originated in Central or Eastern African countries. Recent travel-associated clade I monkeypox cases have almost exclusively been attributed to subclade Ib; there have been no deaths associated with these cases and available data have detailed relatively mild disease courses. Outbreak overview In the United States The risk posed by the clade I monkeypox outbreak to most people within the United States remains low. The risk to men who have sex with men is low to moderate. Clade I cases in the United States have increased in frequency since March 2026. As of October 2026, there have been more than 90 clade I monkeypox cases reported in at least 23 states plus Puerto Rico. The majority of these cases are among gay, bisexual, or other men who have sex with men. Many of these cases: Had recently traveled from areas associated with the outbreak in Central and Eastern Africa; Reported sexual contact during travel to parts of Western Europe where newer outbreaks are being reported; OR Reported sexual contact with people who had traveled from these areas. Since August 2026, an increasing proportion of clade I cases in the United States have been reported in individuals with no recent international travel or known links to people who had recently traveled. Cases with presumed domestic transmission have been reported in more than 10 jurisdictions, indicating a likely transition to sustained clade Ib transmission in the United States. CDC expects to see additional cases of both travel-related and domestically acquired cases of clade Ib occurring across the United States. Preventing Monkeypox While Traveling Learn where monkeypox is spreading and how to lower your risk of getting it during travel. In Central and Eastern Africa Clade Ia monkeypox outbreaks began impacting western areas of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) in 2024. Available data indicate that clade Ia monkeypox has spread through sexual contact, regular household contact, and encounters with infected dead or live wild animals. Clade Ib monkeypox cases initially impacted eastern areas of DRC in late 2023. This subclade has driven much of the spread throughout Central and Eastern Africa and most travel-associated cases to other parts of the world. Available data indicate that these cases at first spread through heterosexual intimate or sexual contact between adults, then spread likely occurred within households, including to children. The disease spread in 2024 and 2025 to neighboring countries of DRC, only some of which are endemic for the virus that causes monkeypox, and sustained local spread occurred. Outside Central and Eastern Africa Several countries in other parts of Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, and South America, as well as Australia, have confirmed clade I monkeypox cases mostly in travelers who'd recently been in areas with clade I outbreaks. To date, there have been more than 300 clade Ib cases in high-income countries, and no deaths have been reported. Several countries in Western Europe are reporting clade Ib monkeypox cases among individuals who had no documented history of international travel. These cases were likely related to intimate or sexual exposure among men who have sex with men. We expect additional cases in Europe and the United States. For data since January 1, 2024, see 2022-24 Monkeypox (Monkeypox) Outbreak: Global Trends (shinyapps.io). Confirmed cases include those that are laboratory confirmed as monkeypox virus and may include cases only confirmed as orthopoxvirus. These data are provided for situational awareness and are subject to change. Clade I monkeypox and children Early in the Central and Eastern African outbreak that began in 2024, a high proportion of suspected cases in some areas were reported in children younger than 15 years of age. Historically, cases of monkeypox in children where monkeypox occurs regularly often happen after contact with wild animals that carry the disease. People then can spread the disease to close contacts, including in their households. The number of children with suspected monkeypox in this outbreak is probably due to spread within and between close households, the large number of younger people in affected countries, misdiagnosis of other diseases (such as measles), poor sanitary conditions, limited access to health services, and a high level of food insecurity and malnutrition. Based on what we know today, we don't expect to see the same sort of pattern in the United States for several reasons, including smaller household sizes, access to cleaning and disinfecting products, and improved access to medical care, equipment, and information about preventing spread while caring for patients. Notably, there are no consistent reports of schools as drivers of monkeypox transmission during any recent outbreaks. If cases of clade I monkeypox do happen in children the United States, CDC has clinical and public health guidance for reporting, testing, and effective case management. What CDC is doing CDC has been supporting DRC monkeypox research and response for more than 20 years. CDC and other U.S. government agencies have continued working in-country with partners in affected areas on disease surveillance, building local laboratory capacity, strengthening public health and laboratory workforce capacity, case investigation, case management, infection prevention and control, border health, and risk communication and community engagement. In the United States, CDC monitors both clade II and clade I monkeypox cases across the United States and follows surveillance reports to monitor for any changes in demographics, disease severity, or case counts by jurisdiction. CDC also performs surveillance testing to identify monkeypox clade for specimens sent to CDC's poxvirus laboratory. CDC staff brought laboratory supplies and trained local staff on laboratory equipment Resources Global Monkeypox: Current Situation There are outbreaks of clade I and clade II monkeypox around the world. View five news elements
Time
The outbreak began in late 2023 in Central and Eastern Africa, with increased frequency in the United States since March 2026. As of October 2026, the outbreak is ongoing.
Location and population
Central and Eastern Africa, particularly the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), and the United States, with cases reported in at least 23 states plus Puerto Rico. The majority of cases in the U.S. are among gay, bisexual, or other men who have sex with men.
Event
Outbreaks of clade I monkeypox have occurred in Central and Eastern Africa since late 2023, with newer outbreaks in Western European countries likely related to this ongoing outbreak. In the United States, clade I monkeypox cases have increased since March 2026, with a transition to sustained clade Ib transmission observed in multiple jurisdictions.
Scale, impact and risk
Since January 2024, nearly 60,000 confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported globally, including more than 150 deaths. In the U.S., as of October 2026, over 90 cases have been reported in 23 states and Puerto Rico. The risk to most people in the U.S. remains low, but it is low to moderate for men who have sex with men. No deaths have been reported in high-income countries for clade Ib cases.
Response, status and uncertainty
CDC is monitoring the outbreak, providing clinical and public health guidance for case management, testing, and prevention. The risk posed by clade I monkeypox to most people in the U.S. remains low, and the spread is being tracked with ongoing data collection and analysis.

Four new mpox cases identified in South Africa - South African Government News Agency

News Brief
The South African Government News Agency reported on 18 October 2026 that four new mpox cases were identified in South Africa. The report does not provide additional details on the number of deaths, exposures, or risk levels associated with these cases. The agency's statement highlights the identification of the cases but does not elaborate on the investigation, testing, or response measures taken. This information is part of the broader news coverage from the agency, which includes various topics such as economic development, democracy, and public safety. The mpox cases are noted as a specific event within the news, but no further details are given regarding the spread or health-system impact of the disease.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

ACAPS 简报:刚果民主共和国 - 猴痘 暴发 影响开赛省(2025 年 10 月 13 日) - ReliefWeb

ACAPS Briefing Note: Democratic Republic of Congo - Mpox outbreak affecting Kasaï province (13 October 2025) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报刚果民主共和国开赛省于 2026 年 9 月 16 日宣布爆发 猴痘 暴发,截至 2026 年 10 月 1 日,报告病例数为 1,017 例,死亡人数超过 24 人。暴发 最初影响了 5 个卫生区,其中 Luebo 卫生区的 Kambangoma 和 Mukwandjanga 是主要地点。当局依赖国家生物医学研究所的实验室结果来进行 诊断,医院工作人员指出资源有限。国家卫生当局于 2026 年 10 月 4 日向奇卡帕部署了快速干预小组,以支持当地的努力。暴发 与麻疹和黄热病 暴发 同时发生,增加了卫生系统的压力。截至 2026 年 10 月 9 日,该省尚未报告埃博拉病例。消息来源并未确认 暴发 是由于当地 传播 还是新传入的,并且 2026 年 4 月国家 猴痘 紧急指定的结束并不一定表明 传播 的停止。
查看新闻五要素
时间
开赛省的 猴痘 暴发 最初于 2026 年 9 月 16 日宣布,截至 2026 年 10 月 1 日报告了 1,017 例病例和超过 24 例死亡。2026 年 10 月 4 日,国家卫生当局向奇卡帕部署了快速干预小组。据报道,暴发 截至 2026 年 10 月 9 日仍在进行中。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在开赛省,特别是 Luebo 卫生区,其中 Kambangoma 和 Mukwandjanga 是主要受影响地点。受影响人群包括两到三个月大的婴儿,该省还同时出现麻疹和黄热病 暴发。
事件
2026 年 9 月 16 日,开赛省宣布了 猴痘 暴发。截至 2026 年 10 月 1 日,所有 18 个卫生区报告了 1,017 例病例和超过 24 例死亡。据报道,暴发 仍在进行中,国家卫生当局于 2026 年 10 月 4 日向奇卡帕部署了快速干预小组。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 产生了重大影响,报告了 1,017 例病例和超过 24 例死亡。同时发生的麻疹和黄热病 暴发 增加了卫生应对能力的压力。消息人士并未证实 暴发 是由本地 传播 引起还是新传入,截至 2026 年 10 月 9 日,该省尚未报告埃博拉病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
卢埃博当局正在等待国家生物医学研究所的实验室结果。医院工作人员报告设备和响应资源有限。国家卫生当局于 2026 年 10 月 4 日向奇卡帕部署快速干预小组,以支持省卫生部门。消息人士没有确定省级测试安排是否发生了变化。
A high priorityPublic-health news

ACAPS Briefing Note: Democratic Republic of Congo - Mpox outbreak affecting Kasaï province (13 October 2025) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
In Kasaï province, Democratic Republic of Congo, an mpox outbreak was declared on 16 September 2026, with 1,017 cases and over 24 deaths reported by 1 October 2026. The outbreak initially affected five health zones, with Kambangoma and Mukwandjanga in Luebo health zone being the main locations. Authorities were relying on laboratory results from the National Biomedical Research Institute for diagnosis, and hospital staff noted limited resources. National health authorities deployed Rapid Intervention Teams to Tshikapa on 4 October 2026 to support local efforts. The outbreak coincided with simultaneous measles and yellow fever outbreaks, increasing strain on health systems. No cases of Ebola were reported in the province as of 9 October 2026. The sources do not confirm whether the outbreak is due to local transmission or a new introduction, and the end of the national mpox emergency designation in April 2026 did not necessarily indicate the cessation of transmission.

Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo ACAPS Briefing Note: Democratic Republic of Congo - Mpox outbreak affecting Kasaï province (13 October 2025) Format Situation Report Source ACAPS Posted 13 Oct 2026 Originally published 13 Oct 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 1.11 MB) CRISIS IMPACT OVERVIEW • On 16 September 2026, provincial authorities announced an mpox outbreak in Kasaï province, initially covering five health zones (7Sur7.cd 17/09/2026; mediacongo. net 17/09/2026). By 1 October, 1,017 cases and more than 24 deaths had been reported across all 18 of the province’s health zones (Actualite.cd 01/10/2026). • The declaration of a provincial mpox outbreak in Kasaï comes five months after the Government announced the end of a national mpox emergency designation; on 2 April, the Government announced a shift from emergency response to resilience, promoting community vigilance given the risk of resurgence (MSPHP 08/04/2026). The end of the emergency designation did not mean that transmission had ceased throughout the country, and available reporting does not establish whether the Kasaï outbreak is a result of continued local transmission or a new introduction into the province. • Earlier media reporting from 1–2 September quoting health sources identified Kambangoma and Mukwandjanga in Luebo health zone as the main affected locations, approximately 20km from the health zone coordination office. Authorities in Luebo were awaiting laboratory results from the National Biomedical Research Institute, indicating reliance on diagnostic support outside the health zone at that stage (ACP 01/09/2026; Radio Okapi 02/09/2026). The sources reviewed do not establish whether provincial testing arrangements have subsequently changed. • Hospital staff reported limited equipment and response resources from the outset. Suspected cases included infants aged two to three months. There were also reports of concerns about obtaining food and lodging during hospital care discouraging patients from seeking treatment (ACP 01/09/2026). • On 4 October, national health authorities deployed Rapid Intervention Teams to Tshikapa to support the Kasaï Provincial Health Division, particularly in Luebo. The mission focuses on epidemiological surveillance, the investigation and early detection of suspected cases, case management, and community awareness, while also reinforcing the operational capacity of local health teams (CongoCroissance 05/10/2026). • Kasaï is facing a simultaneous measles outbreak and suspected cases of yellow fever, increasing pressure on surveillance and health response capacity (WHO 31/08/2026; Beto 23/09/2026). At 9 October, no cases of Ebola had been reported in the province (CDC 09/10/2026). • On 26 September, an mpox case was confirmed in Bunia, the capital of Ituri province; by 29 September, there were five confirmed cases reported in Rwampara and Aru health zones of the province (MedAfrik Info Instagram 26/09/2026; mediacongo. net 01/10/2026; Reuters 25/09/2026). Earlier reports dating from July indicate nearly 4,000 cases confirmed in Sud-Kivu since the start of the year (mediacongo.net 23/07/2026). The available reporting does not establish whether cases in Ituri, Kasaï, and Sud-Kivu are linked to the same transmission chains. Time
The mpox outbreak in Kasaï province was initially announced on 16 September 2026, with 1,017 cases and over 24 deaths reported by 1 October 2026. On 4 October 2026, national health authorities deployed Rapid Intervention Teams to Tshikapa. The outbreak was reported as ongoing as of 9 October 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in Kasaï province, specifically in Luebo health zone, where Kambangoma and Mukwandjanga were the main affected locations. The affected population included infants aged two to three months, and the province was also experiencing simultaneous measles and yellow fever outbreaks.
Event
A mpox outbreak was declared in Kasaï province on 16 September 2026. By 1 October 2026, 1,017 cases and more than 24 deaths had been reported across all 18 health zones. The outbreak was reported as ongoing, with national health authorities deploying Rapid Intervention Teams to Tshikapa on 4 October 2026.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak had a significant impact, with 1,017 cases and over 24 deaths reported. The simultaneous measles and yellow fever outbreaks increased pressure on health response capacity. The sources do not confirm whether the outbreak is due to local transmission or a new introduction, and no cases of Ebola were reported in the province as of 9 October 2026.
Response, status and uncertainty
Authorities in Luebo were awaiting laboratory results from the National Biomedical Research Institute. Hospital staff reported limited equipment and response resources. National health authorities deployed Rapid Intervention Teams to Tshikapa on 4 October 2026 to support the provincial health division. The sources do not establish whether provincial testing arrangements have changed.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

宿主 基因研究揭示猴痘机制并指出药物的再利用 - Bioengineer.org

Host Gene Study Reveals Monkeypox Mechanisms and Points to Repurposed Drugs - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Sabkat Mahmud、Alvira Ajadee 及其同事在 PLOS 被忽视的热带疾病上发表的一项新计算研究揭示了猴痘病毒的关键机制,并确定了其 治疗 的潜在重新利用药物。这项研究于 2026 年进行,研究了人类细胞如何对猴痘感染做出反应,并绘制了差异表达基因图谱,从而鉴定出了与该疾病的 发病机制 相关的 6 个关键 宿主 基因。这些发现表明该病毒可能影响 免疫应答 调节、上皮完整性和代谢适应。该研究还筛选了针对猴痘相关蛋白质的现有药物,重点介绍了五种候选药物:特考韦瑞、Beclabuvir、Maraviroc、Raltegravir 和 Brincidofovir。然而,所有结果均基于计算推断,需要实验验证才能为临床实践提供信息。该研究强调需要进一步研究猴痘的治疗方案,特别是考虑到该病毒自 2022 年以来已传播到其传统的非洲据点之外。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年10月17日星期六。时间范围:2022-2026。
地点与对象
该研究探讨了猴痘在非洲传统据点之外的传播,影响到全世界人口的问题。
事件
Sabkat Mahmud、Alvira Ajadee 及其同事在 PLOS 被忽视的热带疾病上发表的一项计算研究绘制了猴痘病毒的机制,并确定了其 治疗 的潜在重新利用药物。这是一个研究事件,而不是 暴发,并且是根据研究结果得到确认的。
规模、影响与风险
该研究强调,目前还没有正式批准的专门针对猴痘的药物,这表明治疗上存在巨大差距。它确定了五种可能重新用于 治疗 的候选药物,但这些结果基于计算推断,需要实验验证。证据中没有量化影响的规模,但注意到了 治疗 选项不足的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究还处于初步阶段,因为所有结果都基于计算推理,必须经过实验验证。研究小组利用转录组数据、网络生物学和分子模拟来分析病毒的机制和药物相互作用。该研究结果尚未证实可用于临床。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Host Gene Study Reveals Monkeypox Mechanisms and Points to Repurposed Drugs - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A new computational study published in PLOS Neglected Tropical Diseases by Sabkat Mahmud, Alvira Ajadee, and colleagues has revealed key mechanisms of the monkeypox virus and identified potential repurposed drugs for its treatment. The research, conducted in 2026, examined how human cells respond to monkeypox infection and mapped differentially expressed genes, leading to the identification of six key host genes involved in the disease's pathogenesis. These findings suggest that the virus may impact immune response regulation, epithelial integrity, and metabolic adaptation. The study also screened existing drugs against proteins implicated in monkeypox, highlighting five candidates: Tecovirimat, Beclabuvir, Maraviroc, Raltegravir, and Brincidofovir. However, all results are based on computational inference and require experimental validation before they can inform clinical practice. The research underscores the need for further investigation into therapeutic options for monkeypox, especially as the virus has spread beyond its traditional African strongholds since 2022.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, October 17, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Host Gene Study Reveals Monkeypox Mechanisms and Points to Repurposed Drugs by October 17, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Host Gene Study Reveals Monkeypox Mechanisms and Points to Repurposed Drugs Order DNA Kits Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram When monkeypox virus, now widely referred to as MPOX, began spreading beyond its traditional African strongholds in 2022, the world was forced to reconsider a disease long classified as a neglected tropical condition. What had once been a largely localized public health concern rapidly became a global problem, exposing glaring gaps in preparedness, therapeutics, and public awareness. Although vaccines originally developed against smallpox offer some cross-protection, most of the world’s population remains unvaccinated, whether because of limited availability, hesitancy, or simple lack of awareness. More striking still is the absence of any officially approved drug specifically targeting MPOX. A new computational study published in PLOS Neglected Tropical Diseases by Sabkat Mahmud, Alvira Ajadee, and colleagues addresses this therapeutic vacuum by combining host transcriptomics, network biology, and molecular simulation to map the disease’s inner workings and to nominate existing drugs that might be redirected against it. The research team’s strategy rested on a deceptively simple premise: rather than studying the virus in isolation, they examined how human cells respond to infection. Viruses are obligate hijackers, and the genes a host cell switches on or off during infection often reveal the molecular levers the pathogen pulls. The investigators mined three independent transcriptomic profile datasets comparing gene expression in MPOX patients against healthy controls. By intersecting the results across datasets, they filtered out noise and arrived at 166 common host differentially expressed genes, or hDEGs, that consistently distinguish infected individuals from uninfected ones. Of these, 75 genes were upregulated, meaning their activity increased during infection, while 91 were downregulated, suggesting suppression of functions the virus may find inconvenient. Identifying differentially expressed genes is only the first step in untangling a disease process. To separate genuine drivers from incidental bystanders, the team turned to protein-protein interaction network analysis, a technique that maps the web of physical and functional contacts among the proteins encoded by the altered genes. Genes sitting at highly connected nodes of such networks often exert outsized influence on cellular behavior. From this analysis, six top-ranked host key genes emerged as potential MPOX-causing genes: ALB, HSP90AA1, CDH1, PTPRC, IGF1, and ABL1. Each tells a plausible story. ALB encodes serum albumin, the body’s most abundant plasma protein and a marker of liver function and fluid balance. HSP90AA1 is a molecular chaperone that stabilizes many client proteins, including those involved in immune signaling. CDH1 produces E-cadherin, a cell adhesion molecule critical to maintaining epithelial barriers, the very front line that poxviruses breach. PTPRC, better known as CD45, is a tyrosine phosphatase essential for lymphocyte activation, pointing to immune cell involvement. IGF1 governs growth factor signaling, and ABL1 is a kinase with well-characterized roles in cell proliferation and DNA damage responses. Order DNA Kits Discover more Drugs Drug Save on Rx drugs drug With these six hub genes in hand, the researchers performed functional enrichment analysis to determine which biological processes, molecular functions, cellular components, and signaling pathways the genes collectively implicate. Such enrichment analysis asks whether a set of genes is overrepresented in known functional categories compared with what chance would predict. The results painted a coherent picture of MPOX pathogenesis, highlighting processes tied to immune response regulation, epithelial integrity, and metabolic adaptation. The team also extended the analysis to KEGG pathways, a curated database of molecular interaction and reaction networks, which helped situate the hub genes within broader cellular circuits. Complementing this, a regulatory network analysis identified transcription factors and microRNAs that potentially control the expression of the key genes, adding another layer of mechanistic insight. Transcription factors are proteins that switch genes on or off, while microRNAs are small RNA molecules that fine-tune gene expression after transcription; mapping these regulators helps explain how the observed expression changes might arise during infection. Order DNA Kits The second half of the study pivoted from understanding the disease to treating it. Rather than waiting years for a novel antiviral to be discovered and tested, the team pursued drug repurposing, the strategy of finding new uses for approved or well-characterized compounds. Repurposing offers a substantial speed advantage because existing drugs already have established safety profiles, manufacturing routes, and, in many cases, clinical experience in humans. The researchers screened candidate molecules against the proteins implicated in MPOX using molecular docking, a computational method that predicts how strongly and in what orientation a small molecule binds to a target protein’s binding site. From this screen, five top-ranked drug candidates emerged: Tecovirimat, Beclabuvir, Maraviroc, Raltegravir, and Brincidofovir. Discover more Check Symptoms Read Med Journals Genes Several of these nominations carry intriguing logic. Tecovirimat is an antiviral originally developed against smallpox that inhibits the VP37 envelope protein of orthopoxviruses, the viral family that includes monkeypox, and it has already seen emergency use during recent outbreaks. Brincidofovir is a lipid conjugate of cidofovir designed to accumulate in cells and disrupt viral DNA replication, and it too carries orthopoxvirus indications. Maraviroc, by contrast, is an HIV entry inhibitor that blocks the CCR5 receptor on immune cells, while Raltegravir is an HIV integrase strand transfer inhibitor, and Beclabuvir is an investigational hepatitis C NS5A inhibitor. That compounds developed for entirely different pathogens dock favorably onto host or viral proteins relevant to MPOX illustrates the core promise of repurposing: molecular interfaces evolved for one purpose can sometimes be exploited for another. Computational docking alone can be misleading, however, because a molecule that fits a binding site in a static snapshot may fail to maintain that interaction under the dynamic conditions of a living cell. To address this, the team evaluated the pharmacokinetic credentials of all five candidates using ADME/T analysis, which assesses absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion, and toxicity, alongside drug-likeness filters that compare molecular properties against those of successful oral medicines. The candidates passed these evaluations with favorable profiles, suggesting that, in principle, they could reach their targets in the body at effective concentrations without unacceptable toxicity. The strongest computational evidence came from molecular dynamics simulations, which model the physical motion of atoms over time to test whether a drug-protein complex remains stable in a realistic, fluctuating environment. The researchers ran 100-nanosecond simulations of the three most promising complexes: ALB bound to Tecovirimat, CDH1 bound to Maraviroc, and PPARG bound to Beclabuvir. PPARG, the nuclear receptor targeted by Beclabuvir in this analysis, adds an interesting metabolic dimension to the proposed mechanism. Throughout the simulations, the team tracked root-mean-square deviation, or RMSD, which measures how far the protein structure drifts from its starting conformation, and root-mean-square fluctuation, or RMSF, which quantifies how much individual residues wiggle locally. They also computed binding free energies using the MM-GBSA method, which estimates the thermodynamic favorability of the drug-protein interaction. All three complexes demonstrated strong binding stability across these metrics, indicating that the docked poses were not artifacts of a single frozen structure but held up under sustained atomic motion. The convergence of transcriptomic evidence, network analysis, docking, pharmacokinetic screening, and dynamic simulation gives the study an unusually complete computational arc, moving from disease mechanism to therapeutic nomination within a single framework. The identification of host key genes such as ALB, CDH1, and PTPRC may also inform diagnostic development, since consistently dysregulated host transcripts can serve as biomarkers distinguishing MPOX from other febrile illnesses, a persistent challenge in regions where poxvirus, malaria, and other infections overlap clinically. Yet the authors are careful to frame their findings as a starting point rather than a conclusion. Every result in the study, from the 166 differentially expressed genes to the stability of the simulated drug complexes, derives from computational inference and must be validated experimentally in the laboratory before it can inform clinical practice. Docking scores do not guarantee efficacy in infected cells, and transcriptomic correlations do not prove causation. Still, in a landscape where no approved MPOX drug exists and vaccine coverage remains sparse, computational pipelines like this one offer a rapid, systematic way to prioritize which existing molecules deserve the first laboratory look. As MPOX continues to circulate globally, such approaches may shorten the path from genomic data to actionable therapeutics, turning the growing archive of patient transcriptomes into a roadmap for fighting a disease the world can no longer afford to neglect. Time
Publication date: Saturday, October 17, 2026. Time range: 2022-2026.
Location and population
The study addresses the spread of monkeypox beyond its traditional African strongholds, affecting a worldwide population.
Event
A computational study published in PLOS Neglected Tropical Diseases by Sabkat Mahmud, Alvira Ajadee, and colleagues mapped the mechanisms of monkeypox virus and identified potential repurposed drugs for its treatment. This is a research event, not an outbreak, and is confirmed based on the study's findings.
Scale, impact and risk
The study highlights the absence of officially approved drugs specifically targeting monkeypox, indicating a significant therapeutic gap. It identifies five drug candidates that may be repurposed for treatment, but these results are based on computational inference and require experimental validation. The scale of impact is not quantified in the evidence, but the risk of inadequate treatment options is noted.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is in the preliminary stage, as all results are based on computational inference and must be validated experimentally. The research team used transcriptomic data, network biology, and molecular simulation to analyze the virus's mechanisms and drug interactions. The findings are not yet confirmed for clinical use.

南非确认新增四例 猴痘 病例,西开普省仍是受灾最严重的地区 - 开普敦 ETC

South Africa confirms four new mpox cases as Western Cape remains hardest hit - Cape Town ETC
新闻简报南非已确认 4 例新的 猴痘 病例,使 2026 年初以来全国病例总数达到 26 例。其中西开普省占其中 18 例,其中 16 例与当地 猴痘 相关,2 例涉及外国人。受影响人口包括25名男性,年龄介乎23岁至50岁,平均年龄为37岁。卫生署呼吁公众保持警惕,一旦出现皮疹、发烧或疲劳等症状,应立即求医。卫生当局正在重点关注识别感染、防止进一步传播和追踪接触者。该病毒分为多个分支,其中 13 例与分支 Ib 相关,4 例与分支 IIb 相关。当地的 传播 仍然是一个关键问题,政府正在与国家传染病研究所 (NICD) 和省级卫生部门合作,加强 监测 和应对工作。建议公众避免与感染者密切接触,不要共用个人物品,以降低感染风险。

时间
南非新增 4 例 猴痘 感染病例,自 2026 年初以来全国感染总数达到 26 例。最新数据显示,五个省份均发现了 猴痘 病例,其中西开普省报告的感染病例明显多于该国其他地区。发布日期为2026年10月18日。

西开普省是受灾最严重的地区,全国共有26例确诊病例,其中18例为确诊病例。其中16例与当地传播有关,另外2例涉及外国人。受影响人口包括25名年龄在23岁至50岁之间的男性,中位年龄为37岁。
事件
南非新增4例猴痘病例,其中西开普省占确诊病例的大多数。卫生署敦促公众对症状保持警惕并尽早就医。
规模、影响与风险
五个省份均发现了 猴痘 病例,其中西开普省感染人数最多。当地的 传播 是一个关键问题,卫生当局正在重点关注识别感染并防止进一步传播。明确指出该风险与密切身体接触和受污染的个人物品有关。

卫生当局正在继续努力识别感染、预防 传播 并追踪接触者。卫生部正在与国家传染病研究所 (NICD)、省级卫生当局和其他利益相关者合作,加强 监测 并监测病毒的传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

South Africa confirms four new mpox cases as Western Cape remains hardest hit - Cape Town ETC

News Brief
South Africa has confirmed four new mpox cases, bringing the national total to 26 since the start of 2026. The Western Cape province accounts for 18 of these cases, with 16 linked to local transmission and two involving foreign nationals. The affected population consists of 25 males aged 23 to 50, with a median age of 37. The Department of Health has urged the public to remain vigilant and seek medical attention promptly if symptoms such as a rash, fever, or fatigue appear. Health authorities are focusing on identifying infections, preventing further spread, and tracing contacts. The virus is classified into clades, with 13 cases associated with Clade Ib and four with Clade IIb. Local transmission remains a key concern, and the government is collaborating with the National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD) and provincial health authorities to enhance surveillance and response efforts. The public is advised to avoid close contact with infected individuals and not share personal items to reduce the risk of infection.
Original source text (English)
South Africa has recorded four additional mpox infections, bringing the national total to 26 since the beginning of 2026, with the Western Cape accounting for the overwhelming majority of confirmed cases, Cape {town} Etc reports. The Department of Health has urged the public to remain alert to symptoms and seek medical attention early, as health authorities continue efforts to identify infections and prevent further transmission. Of the 26 confirmed cases, 18 have been recorded in the Western Cape, highlighting the importance of continued monitoring and public awareness in the province. The latest figures show that mpox cases have been detected in five provinces, with the Western Cape reporting significantly more infections than the rest of the country. Gauteng has recorded three cases, while the Eastern Cape and Free State have reported two each. KwaZulu-Natal has one confirmed case. According to the Department of Health , 16 of the Western Cape’s 18 cases are linked to local transmission. The other two involved foreign nationals who contracted the virus outside South Africa. The figures indicate that local transmission is a key concern for health authorities, making early detection and the identification of people who may have been exposed important parts of the response. Who is most affected? Of the 26 people diagnosed with mpox, 25 are reportedly male. Their ages range from 23 to 50, with a median age of 37. Laboratory information on the virus’s clade and lineage is available for 17 cases. Of these, 13 are associated with Clade Ib and four with Clade IIb. These classifications help health authorities understand which variants are involved in the outbreak and support efforts to monitor how the virus is spreading. Symptoms to watch for Department of Health spokesperson Foster Mohale has urged people not to ignore possible signs of infection. ‘We urge members of the public to remain vigilant and seek healthcare promptly if they develop symptoms such as a rash, fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes or fatigue.’ – Foster Mohale, Department of Health spokesperson Mpox can spread through close physical contact , including skin-to-skin contact with an infected person. Contact with contaminated bedding, towels, clothing and other personal items can also pose a risk. Anyone who develops a suspicious rash or other symptoms should seek medical advice, particularly if they have recently been in close contact with someone who may have the virus. People who have been exposed to a confirmed or suspected case are advised to inform healthcare workers and monitor themselves for symptoms for 21 days. ‘If symptoms develop, they should avoid close contact with others and seek medical attention,’ Mohale said. Prevention remains a priority The Department of Health says mpox is preventable and treatable, and is urging South Africans to take precautions to reduce the risk of infection. Avoiding close contact with anyone who has an mpox-like rash and not sharing personal items with an infected person are among the recommended measures. Healthcare workers have also been urged to remain alert to possible cases and follow infection prevention and control procedures when assessing patients. The department is working with the National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD) , provincial health authorities and other stakeholders to strengthen surveillance, identify new infections and trace contacts. With most confirmed cases concentrated in the Western Cape, public awareness and timely reporting of symptoms remain important tools in limiting further spread. Mpox Vaccine Slashes Infection Risk by 82%: Real-World Data Details Protection Levels - cristinaiglesias.com

Mpox Vaccine Slashes Infection Risk by 82%: Real-World Data Details Protection Levels - cristinaiglesias.com

News Brief
The article reports that a complete two-dose vaccination schedule for the mpox virus slashes the risk of infection by 82% and limits viral spread within the body by 87%. These findings are based on clinical and epidemiological evaluations, which show that the standard immunisation protocol generates sustained neutralising antibodies. The evidence also highlights that vaccinated individuals who encounter breakthrough infections are less likely to experience systemic dissemination of the virus. Public health officials advise completing the two-dose regimen, monitoring for symptoms, and isolating to prevent further transmission. The study underscores the importance of full-course immunisation for high-risk demographics and challenges misconceptions about the effectiveness of single-dose vaccination. The data supports the need for vigilance and timely access to clinical services to ensure community transmission remains contained.
Original source text (English)

Celebrities Useful Tips World News Celebrities Useful Tips World News World News 0 Mpox Vaccine Slashes Infection Risk by 82%: Real-World Data Details Protection Levels By Сollin RuggPosted onOctober 17, 2026 Time to Read:-words A complete two-dose vaccination schedule provides robust protection against mpox transmission and severe systemic illness. Completing a two-dose course of the mpox vaccine slashes the risk of infection by 82 per cent, providing a substantial protective barrier against the virus. Furthermore, clinical findings reveal that vaccinated individuals who encounter breakthrough infections are 87 per cent less likely to experience viral spread across other parts of the body. With ongoing surveillance surrounding evolving viral lineages such as clade 1b, international public health authorities continue tracking how existing immunisation strategies perform against severe disease. The core protection offered by the current two-dose regimen significantly restricts outward transmission to close contacts and sexual partners, directly shielding vulnerable networks from sustained infection chains. The biological mechanisms behind this defence explain why full-course immunisation remains vital for high-risk demographics. Clinical Protection Levels and Viral Containment The standard immunisation protocol relies on priming the immune system with two separate inoculations to generate sustained neutralising antibodies. Laboratory and epidemiological evaluations show that completing both doses provides the decisive threshold required to suppress active replication. When an individual contracts the virus after completing their inoculations, systemic dissemination remains tightly suppressed. The 87 per cent reduction in viral dissemination prevents lesions from multiplying across multiple anatomical zones, such as the trunk, genital area, and limbs, transforming what could be an extensive illness into a mild, contained presentation. Vaccination provides a highly effective protective barrier by reducing infection risk and limiting bodily spread. Understanding these biological barriers highlights why a partial, single-dose course fails to deliver long-term protection against exposure. Key Metrics: Two-Dose Vaccine Performance Clinical data demonstrate a marked distinction between individuals who receive complete protection and those left unprotected during an exposure event. Metric Observed Impact Overall Infection Risk Reduction 82% reduction after two doses Suppression of Bodily Viral Spread 87% lower risk of systemic dissemination Dosing Schedule Requirement Two full doses required for peak efficacy Primary Clinical Outcome Prevention of widespread rash and systemic illness These clinical metrics directly challenge several widespread misconceptions regarding how the virus behaves across communities. Myth vs Reality: Public Misconceptions on Transmission and Immunity A widespread assumption suggests that receiving a single vaccine jab offers adequate lifetime defence against viral transmission. The reality is that antibody titers remain suboptimal without the mandatory second dose, leaving the immune system vulnerable to breakthrough infections when facing high-risk exposure. Another prevalent myth claims that vaccination completely eliminates the chance of acquiring a localised infection. In practice, immunised individuals may still encounter the virus, but the immune response rapidly neutralises systemic propagation, effectively preventing secondary transmission chains to partners. To maximise personal protection and safeguard partners, public health officials advise clear preventive steps. Recommended Protection and Response Protocol Complete the Regimen: Ensure both doses of the vaccine are administered according to recommended health service intervals. Monitor for Symptoms: Look out for early indicators such as fever, persistent sore throat, or localised rashes across the genital area and limbs. Seek Prompt Assessment: Contact local sexual health clinics or specialised health hotlines immediately upon noticing unexplained lesions. Isolate and Trace: Refrain from close physical contact and assist health services with partner notification to rapidly halt onward transmission. Maintaining active vigilance and ensuring timely access to clinical services ensures community transmission remains strictly contained. 💡 Staying informed about full immunisation schedules ensures strong defence against emerging viral risks. 🔍 Check your local clinic or sexual health service to verify your eligibility for the recommended two-dose course. 👇 Share your experiences or questions in the comments below to help keep the wider community informed. 从 One Health 监测 到 猴痘 和朊病毒:新研究追踪新发疾病的兴起 - Bioengineer.org

From One Health Surveillance to Mpox and Prions: New Research Tracks the Rise of Emerging Diseases - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 17 日发表的一项新研究探讨了中国青海省低 发病率 环境中 猴痘-HIV 混合感染的流行病学特征和 发病率 动态。这项研究是关于新发疾病的更广泛收集的一部分,重点关注一名与男性发生性行为并出现 39.4 摄氏度发烧的男性。基因组特征证实该病毒属于进化枝 IIb,C.1.1 亚系,最可能的暴露途径是本地网络内的高风险性接触。作者指出,该病例表明沉默的 发病率 可能发生在先前被认为低 发病率 的区域,突出了 监测、诊断 和响应的系统性差距。该研究强调了主动疾病监测和整合基因组测序等技术以加强检测和预防工作的重要性。它还呼吁采取跨学科方法和社区参与,制定针对生态敏感地区疾病 监测 的具体策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 17 日星期六。
地点与对象
中国青海省;低 发病率 设置。
事件
发表了一项研究,探讨中国青海省低 发病率 环境中 猴痘-HIV 混合感染的流行病学特征和 传播 动态。该研究是更广泛的新发疾病研究的一部分。
规模、影响与风险
该研究发现一例男男性行为者中的 猴痘-HIV 双重感染病例,病毒被确认为 clade IIb,C.1.1 亚系。患者无国际旅行史,提示当地传播。作者强调了低 发病率 区域中沉默 发病率 的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究强调需要改进 监测、诊断 和响应系统,以解决检测和管理 猴痘-HIV 双重感染方面的差距。它还强调了基因组表征和了解局部 传播 路线的重要性。

From One Health Surveillance to Mpox and Prions: New Research Tracks the Rise of Emerging Diseases - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A new study published on October 17, 2026, explores the epidemiological characteristics and transmission dynamics of Mpox-HIV co-infection in a low-incidence setting in Qinghai Province, China. The research, part of a broader collection on emerging diseases, focuses on a man who has sex with men and presented with a fever of 39.4 degrees Celsius. Genomic characterization confirmed the virus as clade IIb, C.1.1 sub-lineage, with the most likely exposure route being high-risk sexual contact within a local network. The authors note that this case suggests silent transmission may be occurring in regions previously considered low-incidence, highlighting systemic gaps in surveillance, diagnosis, and response. The study underscores the importance of proactive disease monitoring and the integration of technologies like genomic sequencing to enhance detection and prevention efforts. It also calls for interdisciplinary approaches and community involvement in shaping context-specific strategies for disease surveillance in ecologically sensitive areas.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, October 17, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health From One Health Surveillance to Mpox and Prions: New Research Tracks the Rise of Emerging Diseases by October 17, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 From One Health Surveillance to Mpox and Prions: New Research Tracks the Rise of Emerging Diseases Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A sweeping new collection of studies published in Frontiers in Tropical Diseases is offering a multidimensional view of how infectious diseases emerge, re-emerge, and ripple across ecosystems, economies, and public health systems. The research topic, titled “Exploring the Dynamics of Emerging and Re-Emerging Diseases: From Origins to Impact, Volume II,” brings together work spanning tropical disease surveillance, molecular diagnostics, arboviral outbreaks, SARS-CoV-2 reinfection, prion disease, and Mpox-HIV co-infection, painting a picture of a world where the boundaries between human, animal, and environmental health are increasingly porous. At the heart of the collection is a call to rethink how the world detects and responds to disease threats before they spiral into crises. A review by Abdulsalam and colleagues argues that effective surveillance under a “One Health” framework must be proactive rather than reactive, incorporating climate intelligence and addressing the behavioral and social drivers of disease. The authors emphasize that technologies such as artificial intelligence, genomic sequencing, environmental biosensors, and mobile health platforms are dramatically enhancing the speed and scalability of detection, response, and prevention of cross-species transmission. They also stress the need for interdisciplinary education, policy support, and real-time data sharing to build system-wide resilience, recommending that local communities be involved in shaping context-specific surveillance strategies in ecologically sensitive tropical regions. Complementing this systems-level perspective, a study by Iqrar and colleagues developed and validated a novel multiplex PCR assay capable of simultaneously detecting Anaplasma, Babesia, and Theileria pathogens at the genus level in captive wild ungulates. The researchers designed primer pairs targeting the 18S ribosomal RNA gene for piroplasmids, generating products of 178 base pairs for Theileria and 479 base pairs for Babesia, while a separate primer pair targeting the 16S rRNA gene produced an expected 318 base pair product for Anaplasma. Testing blood samples from 60 captive Gazella bennettii and Axis porcinus across wildlife parks and zoos in Punjab, Pakistan, the team found an overall hemoparasite prevalence of 66.66 percent, with sequencing confirming the results and offering a valuable complementary tool for routine, accurate detection in endangered species. Discover more Find Treatments Check Symptoms Track Outbreaks Study Biology health The collection also ventures into the unsettling territory of prion diseases. A clinical case report by Kotkowski and colleagues details the evaluation of a 72-year-old hunter who developed progressive rapid dementia and startle myoclonus after extensive exposure to cervids in a chronic wasting disease-endemic region. During hospitalization, the patient developed posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome, and magnetic resonance imaging revealed cortical ribboning and diffusion restriction in the basal ganglia. Cerebrospinal fluid biomarkers, including RT-QuIC, total tau, and the 14-3-3 protein, along with post-mortem neuropathology, confirmed sporadic Creutzfeldt-Jakob disease of the MM1 subtype. The authors use the case to raise pointed questions about zoonotic surveillance, particularly for hunters with prolonged exposure to wildlife in regions where chronic wasting disease circulates. Shifting to viral threats, Kuang and colleagues examined the epidemiological characteristics and transmission dynamics of Mpox-HIV co-infection in a low-incidence setting in Qinghai Province, China. The patient, a man who has sex with men, presented with a fever of 39.4 degrees Celsius, and recovery of mpox virus nucleic acid was confirmed from a nasopharyngeal swab. Genomic characterization identified the virus as clade IIb, C.1.1 sub-lineage, with the full designation B.1.3.1.1. Notably, the patient had no history of international travel, leading the authors to conclude that the most likely route of exposure was high-risk sexual contact within a local social or sexual network. This finding suggests that silent transmission may already be occurring in regions previously considered low-incidence, exposing systemic gaps in surveillance, diagnosis, and response. The COVID-19 pandemic’s long tail is also examined. Makam and colleagues retrospectively analyzed reinfection data from patients during the second and third waves of the pandemic, finding that of 458 participants, 62, or 13.54 percent, who had a primary infection were confirmed to have been reinfected. Among these, 60 participants, or 13 percent, were asymptomatic, while 398, or 87 percent, were symptomatic, with fever being the most frequently observed symptom during both primary and reinfection episodes. The study also identified a statistically significant association between infection status and vomiting symptoms, adding to the growing body of evidence on how reinfection dynamics and vaccination status interact. Perhaps the most geographically expansive contribution comes from Carrero and Mosquera, who reviewed the re-emergence of arboviruses across the Andean-Amazonian corridor in Latin America. Analyzing datasets on vector-borne diseases from selected countries between 2023 and 2026, they documented the persistence of malaria in Amazonian areas alongside a sustained increase in arboviral infections such as yellow fever, dengue, and chikungunya. Colombia recorded 132 confirmed human cases of yellow fever between 2024 and 2025, while Ecuador registered 65 deaths from 37,840 cases of dengue. Signs of yellow fever re-emergence were also observed in Venezuela during 2025 and 2026, attributed to sylvatic cycles of the virus. Discover more Get Health Tips Health Read Med Journals public health Public Health The authors attribute the re-emergence of these arboviruses to a convergence of ecological, social, and health factors, including poor health education, vaccine hesitancy, limited access to health services, the infodemic, and weaknesses in epidemiological surveillance and vector control. Their findings underscore how fragile public health infrastructure, when combined with environmental change and human mobility, can create conditions ripe for pathogens to resurge even in regions where they were previously contained. Together, the studies in this collection offer a deeper understanding of the ecological, genetic, environmental, and socioeconomic factors that drive the emergence and re-emergence of infectious diseases. They also highlight the cascading effects these diseases exert on public health, societies, and economies, reinforcing the argument that no single discipline or nation can address these threats in isolation. As the editorial’s authors make clear, the path forward demands sustained cross-sectoral integration, investment in molecular and genomic tools, and a willingness to confront the social and behavioral dimensions of disease transmission. From the jungles of the Amazon to the wildlife parks of Punjab and the clinics of Qinghai, the message is consistent: the next emerging disease is not a question of if, but when, and the world’s surveillance systems must be ready. Location and population
Qinghai Province, China; low-incidence setting.
Event
A study examining the epidemiological characteristics and transmission dynamics of Mpox-HIV co-infection in a low-incidence setting in Qinghai Province, China, was published. The research is part of a broader collection on emerging diseases.
Scale, impact and risk
The study identified a case of Mpox-HIV co-infection in a man who has sex with men, with the virus confirmed as clade IIb, C.1.1 sub-lineage. The patient had no history of international travel, suggesting local transmission. The authors highlight the risk of silent transmission in low-incidence regions.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study emphasizes the need for improved surveillance, diagnosis, and response systems to address gaps in detecting and managing Mpox-HIV co-infection. It also highlights the importance of genomic characterization and understanding local transmission routes.

Mpox cases rise to 26 as four new infections are confirmed - newsnote

News Brief
The Department of Health in South Africa confirmed that the number of laboratory-confirmed mpox cases has risen to 26, with four new infections reported. The cases are distributed across several provinces, including the Western Cape (18 cases), Gauteng (3 cases), the Eastern Cape (2 cases), the Free State (2 cases), and KwaZulu-Natal (1 case). Two of the Western Cape cases involved foreign nationals. The report, published on October 18, 2026, highlights the spread of the virus but does not specify the number of deaths or exposures. The government is addressing the situation through public health measures, though no detailed response actions are outlined in the evidence. The information is based on official health data and does not mention uncertainties or further investigation.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Russia investigates suspected monkeypox outbreak near Moscow - CGTN

News Brief
Russia's health authorities have initiated an epidemiological investigation and taken anti-epidemic measures in response to a suspected monkeypox outbreak in the Moscow Region. Officials announced on October 15 that five individuals who had contact with a suspected carrier were hospitalized for medical observation and diagnostic testing. All patients are in satisfactory condition with no pronounced clinical symptoms. Emergency containment actions, such as contact tracing, laboratory testing, and widespread disinfection, were implemented immediately. No new confirmed cases have been reported as of now. The investigation is ongoing, and the use of advanced in-house diagnostic systems is emphasized to ensure rapid detection of the virus. This development follows earlier reported cases in the area, including a confirmed monkeypox case in Domodedovo in February 2026 and two tourists diagnosed in St. Petersburg after returning from Thailand.
Original source text (Korean)
Language SIGN IN USER Your account Sign out Radio TV Ask China Newsletters RSS EXPLORE MORE English Español Français العربية Русский Documentary CCTV+ Home China World World Asia-Pacific Middle East Europe Americas Africa Politics Business Opinions Sci-Tech Culture Sports Travel Nature Picture Video Live Documentary World 2026.10.16 10:57 GMT+8 Russia investigates suspected monkeypox outbreak near Moscow Updated 2026.10.16 10:57 GMT+8 CGTN Russian health authorities have launched an epidemiological investigation and implemented comprehensive anti-epidemic measures following a suspected monkeypox outbreak in the Moscow Region, officials announced on October 15. According to a public statement released by the Federal Service for Oversight of Consumer Protection and Welfare (Rospotrebnadzor), five people who came into contact with a suspected monkeypox carrier have been hospitalized for medical observation and diagnostic testing. A monkeypox test kit at the State Research Center of Virology and Biotechnology Vector in Novosibirsk, Russia. /CFP The Moscow Region Ministry of Health confirmed that all five patients are in satisfactory condition and show no pronounced clinical symptoms. Authorities said emergency containment measures, including contact tracing, laboratory testing and widespread disinfection, were implemented immediately. Rospotrebnadzor said health officials are using advanced diagnostic systems developed in-house to ensure the rapid detection of the virus. While testing is underway, officials emphasized that no new cases have been confirmed. A syringe lies on a vaccination card marked "Mpox vaccination." /CFP This development follows earlier reported cases in the area. In February 2026, health authorities confirmed a monkeypox case in Domodedovo. A close contact was subsequently hospitalized, and two tourists returning to St. Petersburg from Thailand were later diagnosed with the disease. Monkeypox is a viral zoonotic disease. Initial symptoms in humans include fever, headaches, muscle aches, back pain and swollen lymph nodes. These may be followed by extensive rashes on the face and body. While most infected people recover within a few weeks, some may develop severe illness or die. 俄罗斯因疑似 猴痘 暴露而住院五人,并启动紧急疾病调查 - UNITED24 Media

Russia Hospitalizes Five After Suspected Mpox Exposure, Launches Emergency Disease Investigation - UNITED24 Media
新闻简报据国际文传电讯社报道,2026 年 10 月 15 日,俄罗斯莫斯科地区有 5 人因疑似接触 猴痘病毒 而住院。The Moscow region Health Ministry confirmed the hospitalizations and placed the individuals under medical observation. However,no clinical symptoms of 猴痘 were observed,and test results had not been disclosed. The suspected infection remains unconfirmed.作为回应,俄罗斯消费者健康监管机构 Rospotrebnadzor 启动了流行病学调查,下令采取消毒措施,并开始识别更多接触者,以防止进一步的 传播。The investigation is ongoing,and the status of the suspected case is under review.这一事件是俄罗斯更广泛的公共卫生问题的一部分,包括早些时候确认的 猴痘 感染和西伯利亚据称的鼠疫 暴发,尽管后者与 猴痘 无关。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 15 日,五人因疑似接触 猴痘 而入院。住院和调查报告于2026年10月16日进行。
地点与对象
该事件发生在俄罗斯莫斯科地区,涉及五人,他们与一名疑似感染 猴痘 的人有过接触。
事件
俄罗斯莫斯科地区卫生部确认有五人因疑似 猴痘 暴露而住院治疗,俄罗斯联邦国家局对疑似病例启动了流行病学调查。
规模、影响与风险
五人住院并接受医学观察。患者中未观察到 猴痘 的临床症状,检测结果未披露。疑似感染尚未得到证实,也没有明确的风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局收集样本进行实验室检测,以确定这些人是否感染了 猴痘。已下令采取消毒措施,并正在确定其他接触者,以防止进一步的 传播。调查仍在进行中,疑似病例的状况仍在审查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Russia Hospitalizes Five After Suspected Mpox Exposure, Launches Emergency Disease Investigation - UNITED24 Media

News Brief
In Russia’s Moscow region, five individuals were hospitalized on October 15, 2026, after suspected exposure to the mpox virus, as reported by Interfax. The Moscow region Health Ministry confirmed the hospitalizations and placed the individuals under medical observation. However, no clinical symptoms of mpox were observed, and test results had not been disclosed. The suspected infection remains unconfirmed. In response, Russia’s consumer health watchdog, Rospotrebnadzor, launched an epidemiological investigation, ordered disinfection measures, and began identifying additional contacts to prevent further transmission. The investigation is ongoing, and the status of the suspected case is under review. This event is part of a broader context of public health concerns in Russia, including earlier confirmed mpox infections and an alleged plague outbreak in Siberia, though the latter is unrelated to mpox.

DONATE NOW The ministry noted that all five patients were in satisfactory condition and showed no clinical symptoms of mpox. Their test results had not been disclosed, while the suspected infection that prompted their hospitalization remained unconfirmed. Later on October 15, Russia’s consumer health watchdog, Rospotrebnadzor, announced that it had launched an epidemiological investigation into the suspected case. The agency confirmed that five contacts were hospitalized and that disinfection measures had been ordered. "Specialists from Rospotrebnadzor's Moscow region office immediately launched a sanitary and epidemiological investigation," the agency stated, as reported by Interfax. Officials added that they were identifying additional contacts and implementing measures to prevent further transmission. Read more Category War in Ukraine Russia Hides Potential Pneumonic Plague Outbreak After Fatal Lab Breach Oct 04, 2026 12:45 The development follows several confirmed mpox infections in Russia earlier in 2026. On February 15, RBC reported, citing Rospotrebnadzor, that two men returning from Thailand had tested positive for the virus in St. Petersburg. Both were hospitalized, while authorities placed their contacts under medical observation. The suspected mpox case follows public health concerns in Siberia, where an alleged plague outbreak in the Irkutsk region drew international attention earlier in October. Laboratory worker Darya Shipilova, 28, died on October 2 after reportedly breaking a test tube containing live bacteria at the Irkutsk Anti-Plague Institute, and hundreds of her contacts were placed in quarantine. Russian authorities attributed her death to unidentified pneumonia and denied that an outbreak was occurring. Unconfirmed reports of a second death emerged on October 6, while Mongolia, Kyrgyzstan, and Uzbekistan tightened health controls. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

卫生调查开始后,莫斯科州五人因疑似 猴痘 被隔离 - 乌克兰新声音

Five quarantined in Moscow Oblast over suspected mpox as sanitary probe begins - The New Voice of Ukraine
新闻简报据俄罗斯官方媒体 2026 年 10 月 16 日报道,俄罗斯莫斯科州有 5 人因疑似 猴痘 感染而接受严格的三周隔离。这些患者包括 3 名男性和 2 名女性,年龄在 21 岁至 35 岁之间,于 10 月 10 日直接接触受感染的携带者后入院。俄罗斯消费者和卫生监管局地区管理局确认了这一隔离情况,并启动了卫生流行病学调查进行排查,并采取局部消毒措施。然而,由于实验室样本尚未处理,猴痘 诊断 仍未得到证实。目前,患者临床状况良好,无明显症状。该事件发生前,伊尔库茨克州一名 28 岁的实验室专家不小心打破了含有肺鼠疫病原体的试管,导致死亡。俄罗斯当局否认有任何与鼠疫有关的违规行为,而疾病预防控制中心和美国国务院已对死亡情况和随后的隔离情况展开调查。该报告强调了持续存在的公共卫生问题以及需要进一步调查以确认 猴痘 的存在。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 16 日上午 07:39;事件日期没有明确说明,但隔离是在该日期开始的。根据证据,这些人于 10 月 10 日暴露。
地点与对象
俄罗斯莫斯科州。5名居民被隔离,其中三男两女,年龄在21至35岁之间。
事件
莫斯科州的五名居民因疑似感染猴痘而被隔离。俄罗斯官方媒体报道了这一事件,并得到了消费者和卫生监管机构 Rospotrebnadzor 地区管理局的确认。
规模、影响与风险
五人被隔离,没有死亡或症状的报告。没有明确说明风险,但实施隔离是作为预防措施。实验室诊断样本尚未经过处理,导致 猴痘 诊断 未经确认。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生流行病学调查正在进行中,并已执行局部消毒的正式命令。患者临床状况令人满意,但 诊断 尚未得到证实。消息来源为俄罗斯官方媒体,该报道基于已核实的事件。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Five quarantined in Moscow Oblast over suspected mpox as sanitary probe begins - The New Voice of Ukraine

News Brief
Five individuals in Moscow Oblast, Russia, were placed under strict three-week quarantine due to suspected mpox infection, as reported by Russian state media on October 16, 2026. The patients, comprising three men and two women aged between 21 and 35, were hospitalized after coming into direct contact with an infected carrier on October 10. The regional directorate of consumer and health regulator Rospotrebnadzor confirmed the quarantine and initiated a sanitary-epidemiological investigation, along with localized disinfection measures. However, the mpox diagnosis remains unconfirmed as laboratory samples have not yet been processed. The patients are currently in satisfactory clinical condition without overt symptoms. The incident follows the death of a 28-year-old laboratory specialist in Irkutsk Oblast, who had accidentally broken a test tube containing the pneumonic plague pathogen. Russian authorities have denied any breach related to the plague, while the CDC and U.S. State Department have launched inquiries into the circumstances of the death and subsequent quarantines. The report highlights the ongoing public health concerns and the need for further investigation to confirm the presence of mpox.
Original source text (English)
Exclusive Nation business life opinion Russian war Nation Dear reader! This article will be available right after an advertisement. Five quarantined in Moscow Oblast over suspected mpox as sanitary probe begins October 16, 2026, 07:39 AM 208 World Five residents of Moscow Oblast were placed under strict three-week quarantine (Photo: REUTERS/Anastasia Barashkova) Five residents were hospitalized under quarantine in Moscow Oblast with suspected mpox (Monkeypox virus), Russian state media reported on Oct. 16. The hospitalized patients include three men and two women aged between 21 and 35. Investigators believe the individuals came into direct contact with an infected carrier on Oct. 10, later seeking medical attention. All five patients have been placed into an obligatory three-week clinical quarantine. Read also: U.S. embassy issues health alert over Russia plague risk The regional directorate of consumer and health regulator Rospotrebnadzor verified the incident, confirming that a sanitary-epidemiological investigation is underway and formal orders for comprehensive localized disinfection have been executed. Ad Russia’s Ministry of Health claimed the patients remain in satisfactory clinical condition and currently exhibit no overt symptoms of the virus. However, Russian media noted that laboratory diagnostic samples have not yet been processed, leaving the mpox diagnosis officially unconfirmed. Suspicious Biological Incidents Across Russia Read also: Cause of Russian plague lab worker’s death still unknown Lethal infection at Siberian anti-plague institute: The Moscow Oblast quarantine follows the death of 28-year-old Daria Shipilova, a laboratory specialist at the Anti-Plague Research Institute in Irkutsk Oblast. Russian independent media reported that on Sept. 25, Shipilova accidentally broke a test tube containing the pathogen for pneumonic plague. She was hospitalized with severe pneumonia on Sept. 29 and died overnight into Oct. 2. Covert quarantine orders in Irkutsk: Following Shipilova's death, Russian authorities enacted strict containment protocols across her research laboratory and adjoining medical clinics, locking down staff inside the facilities for three days and placing roughly 200 secondary contacts under surveillance. Officially, Russian authorities claimed Shipilova succumbed to "pneumonia of unknown origin" and vehemently denied any plague breach. U.S. intelligence and diplomatic scrutiny: The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), the U.S. State Department, and allied agencies launched inquiries to determine the circumstances surrounding Shipilova’s death and the subsequent hospital quarantines in Siberia. On Oct. 5, U.S. President Donald Trump publicly described the Siberian outbreak as a "big problem for Russia" and offered technical assistance, subsequently announcing plans for a direct telephone call with Russian dictator Vladimir Putin to address the suspected plague incident. 卫生部门发出警报,四例新的 猴痘 病例使南澳总病例数达到 26 例 - IOL

Health department sounds alarm as four new Mpox cases push SA total to 26 - IOL
新闻简报南非卫生部已确认新增 4 例 猴痘 病例,自 2026 年 1 月 1 日起全国病例总数增至 26 例。其中 18 例来自西开普省,其中 16 例与当地 猴痘 病例有关,另外 2 例是从外国人输入的。26例确诊病例中,25例为男性,年龄在23岁至50岁之间,中位年龄为37岁。有17例病例有分支信息,其中13例与分支Ib相关,4例与分支IIb相关。当局敦促公众避免与感染者密切接触,在接触后21天内监测症状,并及时就医。该部门正在与国家传染病研究所 (NICD)、省级卫生部门和其他利益相关者合作,加强 监测、追踪接触者并防止进一步传播。公众已被警告与受影响的个人密切接触和分享个人物品的风险。卫生部发言人 Foster Mohale 强调保持警惕和及时获得医疗保健对于有效管理 暴发 的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 18 日,星期日。
地点与对象
南非,西开普省占绝大多数感染者,26 名确诊 猴痘 的人中有 25 人是男性,年龄在 23 岁至 50 岁之间。
事件
卫生部确认新增 4 例 猴痘 病例,使自 2026 年 1 月 1 日以来全国病例总数达到 26 例。这些病例由卫生部报告,并由发言人福斯特·莫哈尔 (Foster Mohale) 确认。
规模、影响与风险
自今年年初以来,共记录了 26 例实验室确诊的 猴痘 病例,其中西开普省有 18 例与当地 猴痘 病例有关,另外 2 例是从外国人输入的。该部门强调,猴痘是可预防和可治疗的,并敦促保持警惕,防止进一步传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局正在与国家传染病研究所 (NICD)、省级卫生部门和其他利益相关者合作,加强 监测、识别病例、追踪接触者并提高公众意识。该部门已发出警告,并敦促公众监测症状并避免与感染者密切接触。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health department sounds alarm as four new Mpox cases push SA total to 26 - IOL

News Brief
The Department of Health in South Africa has confirmed four new mpox cases, raising the national total to 26 since January 1, 2026. The Western Cape accounts for 18 of these cases, with local transmission linked to 16 of them and two imported from foreign nationals. Of the 26 confirmed cases, 25 are male, aged between 23 and 50, with a median age of 37. Clade information was available for 17 cases, with 13 associated with Clade Ib and four with Clade IIb. Authorities are urging the public to avoid close contact with infected individuals, monitor symptoms for 21 days after exposure, and seek medical attention promptly. The department is collaborating with the National Institute for Communicable Diseases (NICD), provincial health departments, and other stakeholders to strengthen surveillance, trace contacts, and prevent further spread. The public has been warned about the risks of close contact and sharing personal belongings with affected individuals. The Department of Health spokesperson, Foster Mohale, emphasized the importance of vigilance and timely healthcare access to manage the outbreak effectively.
Original source text (English)
Sunday, October 18, 2026 News Crime and Courts South Africa Africa World BRICS Education Energy Environment Feel Good Government at Work Opinion LGE2026 Politics Opinion Opinion News Politics Entertainment Lifestyle Tech Sport Business Jobs Economy Property Window on China Hybrid Working Entertainment Celebrity News Streaming Movies Music Books Whats On Joburg Durban Cape Town Pretoria Opinion Lifestyle Health Beauty Fashion Food Relationships Parenting Competitions Opinion Travel Local International Tech Software and Internet Esports Opinion Sport Soccer PSL Rugby Cricket MMA HOLLYWOODBETS iGAMING Opinion Horse Racing Motoring Reviews Launches Industry News Motorsport News South Africa Health department sounds alarm as four new Mpox cases push SA total to 26 Xolile Mtembu|Published 13 hours ago Ask AI 2min The Department of Health has issued a warning after four new mpox cases were confirmed in South Africa, bringing the national tally to 26. With 16 Western Cape cases linked to local transmission, authorities are urging people to avoid close contact with infected individuals and monitor symptoms for 21 days after exposure. Image: FILE South Africa has recorded four new Mpox cases, pushing the national tally to 26 since the beginning of the year, with the Western Cape accounting for the overwhelming majority of infections. The Department of Health has urged the public to remain vigilant, recognise the warning signs and seek medical attention promptly to help prevent further transmission of the virus. Spokesperson Foster Mohale confirmed the increase, saying the latest cases had raised the cumulative number of laboratory-confirmed infections from 22 to 26. Western Cape accounts for 18 infections Of the 26 confirmed cases recorded since January 1, 2026, the Western Cape has reported 18, followed by Gauteng with three, the Eastern Cape and Free State with two each, and KwaZulu-Natal with one. Two of the Western Cape cases involved foreign nationals visiting South Africa and were classified as imported infections. The remaining 16 were linked to local transmission. The department said 25 of the 26 people diagnosed with mpox were male, with ages ranging from 23 to 50. The median age was 37. Clade and lineage information was available for 17 cases. Of these, 13 were associated with Clade Ib and four with Clade IIb. Mohale urged people to take precautions and seek help if they suspected they had contracted the virus. “The department urges members of the public to remain vigilant and to seek healthcare promptly if they develop symptoms such as a rash, fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes or fatigue,” he said. Public warned about close contact Mpox is a viral infection that can spread through close contact with an infected person. The department advised anyone who had been in close contact with a confirmed or suspected case to notify healthcare workers and monitor themselves for symptoms for 21 days. People who develop symptoms should avoid close contact with others and seek medical attention. The public has also been warned against close, skin-to-skin contact with anyone who has a rash suspected to be linked to mpox. Sharing personal belongings, including bedding, towels and clothing, with affected individuals should be avoided. Mohale emphasised that people who may have been exposed should monitor their health carefully. “Individuals who have been in close contact with a confirmed or suspected mpox case should inform healthcare workers and monitor themselves for symptoms for 21 days,” he said. 信德省男婴 猴痘 检测呈阳性,病例数上升至 94 例 - Dawn

Infant boy tests positive for mpox in Sindh as tally rises to 94 - Dawn
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 13 日,巴基斯坦信德省报告了一起涉及一名男婴的 猴痘 确诊病例,该省的病例总数升至 94 例。这名婴儿目前在信德省传染病医院和研究中心 (SIDH&RC) 接受 治疗 治疗,最初在兰迪的一家诊所接受检查,然后被转送到医院。省疾病 监测 和反应小组指出,卡拉奇是受影响最严重的城市,有 28 例病例,其次是凯普尔(Khairpur),有 22 例病例,苏库尔(Sukkur)有 9 例病例。受影响最严重的年龄组是30至39岁,其中70例涉及男性患者。两名患者在医院死亡,其中一名患有严重肺部疾病,另一名患有慢性肾脏疾病,但他们的死因是慢性疾病。该医院今年已记录了23例猴痘患者,每月趋势显示,4月病例数最多(31例),其次是9月(20例)和8月(17例)。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于2026年10月15日发布。这名男婴于2026年10月13日星期三晚上检测出猴痘呈阳性。每月趋势数据显示,4月(31)、9月(20)和8月(17)报告了病例。
地点与对象
该病例报告在信德省,具体在卡拉奇和兰迪地区。受影响的总人数为94人,其中23例涉及0至9岁儿童,18例涉及20至29岁成人。75%的患者为男性,24pc为女性。
事件
据省疾病 监测 和反应小组报告,信德省一名男婴 猴痘 检测呈阳性。该病例已得到确认,婴儿目前在信德省传染病医院和研究中心(SIDH&RC)接受治疗治疗。
规模、影响与风险
今年信德省共报告了 94 起 猴痘 病例。两名患者在医院死亡,其中一名患有严重肺部疾病,另一名患有慢性肾脏疾病,但他们的死因是慢性疾病。受影响最严重的城市是卡拉奇,有 28 例病例,其次是凯布尔(Khairpur),有 22 例病例,苏库尔(Sukkur)有 9 例病例。受影响最严重的年龄组是30至39岁,其中70例涉及男性患者。
应对、状态与不确定性
省疾病 监测 和响应单位已发布 猴痘 病例的数据。这名男婴最初在兰迪的一家诊所接受了检查,并转诊至 SIDH&RC。该医院今年已记录了23名猴痘患者,其中包括最新的病例。两名患者的死亡原因被认为与 猴痘 无关。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Infant boy tests positive for mpox in Sindh as tally rises to 94 - Dawn

News Brief
A confirmed mpox case involving an infant boy was reported in Sindh, Pakistan, on October 13, 2026, as the total number of cases in the province rose to 94. The baby, currently under treatment at the Sindh Infectious Diseases Hospital & Research Centre (SIDH&RC), was initially examined at a clinic in Landhi before being referred to the hospital. The Provincial Disease Surveillance and Response Unit noted that Karachi is the most affected city with 28 cases, followed by Khairpur with 22 and Sukkur with nine. The most affected age group is 30 to 39 years, with 70 cases involving male patients. Two patients, one with severe lung illness and the other with chronic kidney disease, died at the hospital, but their causes of death were attributed to their chronic illnesses. The hospital has recorded 23 mpox patients this year, and the monthly trend shows April had the highest number of cases (31), followed by September (20) and August (17).
Original source text (English)
EPAPER LIVE TV DAWNNEWS URDU Images Herald Aurora CityFM89 Advertise Events Supplements Careers Obituaries Dawn E-Paper | October 19, 2026 Home Latest US-Iran War Pakistan Opinion Business Images Prism World Sport Breathe Magazines Tech Videos Popular Archive Flood Donations Home Latest US-Iran War Pakistan Opinion Business Images Prism World Sport Breathe Magazines Tech Videos Popular Archive Flood Donations Search Search Subscribe to notifications Get the latest news and updates from Dawn Not Now Allow Notifications Infant boy tests positive for mpox in Sindh as tally rises to 94 Faiza Ilyas Published October 15, 2026 Email Story Your Name * Recipient Email * Send Email Cancel 0 FILE PHOTO: A test tube labelled "Mpox virus positive" is held in this illustration taken August 20, 2024. — Reuters/File comments Join our Whatsapp Channel KARACHI: The city reported its first mpox case involving an infant boy on Wednesday evening as the total number of people affected by the disease in Sindh this year rose to 94. Sources said the 40-day-old baby boy was currently under treatment at the Sindh Infectious Diseases Hospital & Research Centre (SIDH&RC). The parents are residents of Landhi. Speaking to Dawn on condition of anonymity, a senior SIDH&RC doctor said that the baby boy had initially been examined at a clinic in Landhi four to five days ago, after which he developed fever and rashes all over the body. “The parents took him to a Korangi hospital, from where he was referred to us. He is stable but restless,” he said, adding that 23 mpox patients, including the latest case, had reported at the hospital this year so far. The sources said that two mpox patients, one with severe lung illness and the other with chronic kidney disease, had died at the hospital over the past few weeks. In hospital records, however, their causes of death were attributed to their chronic illnesses rather than mpox. Early this month, the Provincial Disease Surveillance and Response Unit released data showing 11 deaths from mpox this year. It showed that Karachi is the most mpox-affected city with 28 cases, followed by 22 in Khairpur and nine in Sukkur. The monthly trend showed that April saw the largest number of cases (31) this year, followed by September (20) and August (17). Seventy cases involved male patients, and the most affected age group was 30 to 39 years. Twenty-three cases involved children aged zero to nine years across the province, and 18 cases involved adults aged 20-29. Seventy-five per cent of patients were men and 24pc women. In April, an outbreak of mpox, which was earlier restricted to patients with a history of foreign travel, was reported from Khairpur, claiming the lives of several newborns. The babies were under treatment in the intensive care units of two hospitals. Response, status and uncertainty
The Provincial Disease Surveillance and Response Unit has released data on mpox cases. The infant boy was initially examined at a clinic in Landhi and referred to SIDH&RC. The hospital has recorded 23 mpox patients this year, including the latest case. The cause of death for two patients was noted as unrelated to mpox.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health Department confirms 4 more Mpox cases - EWN

News Brief
The South African Health Department confirmed four additional Mpox cases on 18 October 2026, bringing the national total to 26 since the start of the year. The Western Cape reported the most cases (18), followed by Gauteng (3), Eastern Cape and Free State (2 each), and KwaZulu-Natal (1). Of the Western Cape cases, 16 are linked to local transmission, while two involve foreign nationals. The majority of infected individuals are male, aged 23 to 50. The department is urging the public to remain vigilant and seek medical attention if symptoms such as rash, fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes, or fatigue appear. It also advised close contacts of confirmed or suspected cases to monitor themselves for 21 days and avoid close contact if symptoms develop. The response remains ongoing, with no explicit risk level stated, but the department is actively managing the situation through communication and monitoring.

Four new Mpox cases confirmed - eNCA

News Brief
On 18 October 2026, the Health Department confirmed four new Mpox cases, bringing the total number of confirmed cases nationwide to 26. The cases were reported in Gauteng, the Western Cape, the Eastern Cape, the Free State, and KwaZulu-Natal. Two of the cases involve foreign nationals, while the remaining are linked to local transmission. Authorities are urging the public to monitor for symptoms such as a rash, fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes, or fatigue and to seek medical attention if they develop these. Additionally, anyone who has been in contact with a confirmed case is advised to monitor themselves for symptoms for 21 days. The response includes public health advisories, but no specific details on containment measures or resolution have been provided.
