莫斯科附近的猴痘警报:五人住院 - ua-stena.info

Monkeypox alert near Moscow: Five hospitalized - ua-stena.info
新闻简报莫斯科附近发生的疑似猴痘病例促使俄罗斯当局展开调查,五人因接触可能的猴痘病毒携带者而入院治疗。该事件与俄罗斯之前的一起病例有关,但住院患者中尚未发现确诊感染病例。这五名患者(三男两女)正在接受医学观察,并已采集样本进行实验室分析。当局正在进行接触者追踪并采取包括消毒在内的预防措施。然而,由于缺乏确认的检测结果,这意味着情况仍在调查中,风险级别尚未明确说明。俄罗斯卫生官员称患者的状况令人满意,并正在密切监测情况。在获得测试结果之前,该事件不应被视为新 暴发 的证据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
事件日期没有明确说明,但据报道事件发生在 2026 年 10 月上旬。截至 2026 年 10 月 16 日,住院和调查仍在进行中。
地点与对象
该事件发生在俄罗斯莫斯科附近。五人(三男两女)被送往医院接受医学观察。
事件
俄罗斯当局正在调查莫斯科地区一例疑似猴痘病例,五人因接触可能的病毒携带者而入院治疗。疑似携带者的身份是在俄罗斯之前的一起病例中发现的。
规模、影响与风险
目前,尚无确凿证据表明5名住院患者中有人被感染。目前情况正在调查中,尚未说明具体的风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
俄罗斯卫生当局已启动流行病学调查,收集样本进行实验室分析,并正在监测情况。目前情况正在调查中,测试结果正在等待中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Monkeypox alert near Moscow: Five hospitalized - ua-stena.info

News Brief
A suspected monkeypox case near Moscow has prompted Russian authorities to investigate after five individuals were hospitalized following contact with a possible carrier. The incident is linked to a previous case in Russia, but no confirmed infections have been identified among the hospitalized individuals. The five patients, three men and two women, are under medical observation, and samples have been collected for laboratory analysis. Authorities are conducting contact tracing and implementing preventative measures, including disinfection. However, the absence of confirmed test results means the situation remains under investigation, and the risk level has not been explicitly stated. Russian health officials have described the patients' condition as satisfactory and are monitoring the situation closely. Until test results are available, this incident should not be considered evidence of a new outbreak.
Original source text (English)

Home World News Wars and Conflicts Technology UFO & Aliens Historical Mysteries Fascinating but Strange Home › World News › Monkeypox alert near Moscow: Five hospitalized after contact with suspected carrier as doctors investigate possible spread in Russia By Anastasia Prizma / 2026-10-16 / World News Monkeypox alert near Moscow: Five hospitalized Russian authorities are investigating a suspected case of monkeypox in the Moscow region after five people were hospitalized after coming into contact with a possible carrier of the virus. This has raised new concerns, as doctors conduct tests to determine whether the virus has spread. Five patients, three men and two women, have been placed under medical observation. Samples have been collected for laboratory analysis, and regional authorities have launched an epidemiological investigation. There is currently no confirmed evidence that any of the five people have been infected. Russian health authorities described their condition as satisfactory and stated that they are monitoring the situation. According to Russian media reports, the patients had contact with a person suspected of being infected with the virus. Authorities have begun identifying other possible contacts and implementing preventative measures, including disinfection. The absence of symptoms does not automatically rule out infection. Monkeypox, also known as mpox, can have an incubation period of up to 21 days. Therefore, healthcare workers monitor people exposed to the virus for signs of illness. The virus can be spread through close physical contact with an infected person, including contact with skin lesions and bodily fluids. Transmission can also occur through contaminated clothing, towels, or bedding. Prolonged face-to-face contact can be another route of infection. Symptoms can include fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes, and a characteristic rash. The lesions can develop into painful blisters. Many people recover within two to four weeks, although severe illness is possible, especially among vulnerable groups. The suspected case emerged after another case in Russia attracted attention. In early October, the death of a laboratory technician in Siberia prompted precautionary surveillance of people who may have been exposed to dangerous infections. Russian authorities later stated that thousands of tests revealed no signs of plague among those who had been in contact with the infected individual during this investigation. A connection between the two incidents has not yet been established. The monkeypox situation near Moscow remains under investigation. Five hospitalized individuals are under observation, awaiting laboratory test results. Their hospitalization does not necessarily mean they have tested positive. In the coming days, it should become clearer whether the suspected exposure has led to any infections. Until test results are confirmed, this incident should not be considered evidence of a new outbreak. ← Previous Post Next Post → Related Posts EU backs Croatia’s accession to Schengen area Ministers from 27 EU member states voted for this decision. “We did it! Scale, impact and risk

Original title (English)
Five people hospitalized in Moscow region over suspected mpox exposure - Головне в Україні
News Brief
Five individuals in the Moscow region of Russia were hospitalized following suspected exposure to the mpox virus. The report, issued by Interfax and citing the Moscow Region Health Ministry, indicates that tests were conducted on all patients, none of whom currently display clinical symptoms of the disease. The regional health authorities have stated that the situation remains under control. The patients are now under medical observation, and their condition is described as satisfactory. This event highlights the ongoing monitoring and response efforts to mpox cases in the area, though no further details on the source of exposure or the status of the suspected case have been provided. The hospitalization underscores the potential risk of mpox transmission in the region, particularly given the virus's association with Central and West Africa. The response is considered preliminary, with no indication of a broader outbreak at this time.
Original source text (English)

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Four cases of mpox have been confirmed in St Petersburg - UkrMedia News

News Brief
On 16 October 2026, UkrMedia News reported that Russia confirmed four laboratory-confirmed cases of mpox in St Petersburg. The patients were hospitalised on 15 October and are being treated at Botkin Clinical Infectious Diseases Hospital. Their condition is described as moderate. Additionally, five people in the Moscow region who had contact with a suspected carrier of the infection are under medical supervision and are asymptomatic, awaiting test results. Rospotrebnadzor is conducting an epidemiological investigation and has issued an order for disinfection. The situation is considered under control, with no official confirmation of large-scale spread between regions. The information is based on reports from Russian media outlets and preliminary assessments, with test results still pending for the Moscow region cases.

Briefly about the main points Four cases of mpox have been laboratory-confirmed in St Petersburg. Hospitalised patients are being treated at the Botkin Hospital. Those who had been in contact with the patient in Zhukovsky were taken to Ramenskoye Hospital. They have no symptoms or clinical signs of the disease. Rospotrebnadzor is conducting an epidemiological investigation and has issued an order to carry out disinfection. Add UkrMedia to your Google sources Get more of our news in the recommendations. Patients in St Petersburg were taken to an infectious diseases hospital Russian media outlets REN TV, *Kommersant* and local media reported on the hospitalisation. According to SHOT, the people were taken from a residential building on Piskarevsky Prospekt in the Krasnogvardeisky district and transported to the Botkin Clinical Infectious Diseases Hospital. According to initial reports, the patients had a high temperature and swollen lymph nodes. «Fontanka» also reported on the hospitalisation of people with monkeypox, citing its own source; however, at the time of publication, the publication was still clarifying the exact number of cases. People who have been in contact with the case in Zhukovsky are asymptomatic and are awaiting test results The individuals were admitted to hospital on 15 October following contact with a person suspected of having mpox. They are three men and two women who lived in the same flat; the probable exposure to the source of infection took place on 10 October. They are at the Ramenskaya Hospital. The chief medical officer Serhiy Markitan told Interfax that all the patients are in a satisfactory condition. Samples have been taken from them for testing, and the results are due to be provided by Russian Federal Service for Consumer Rights Protection. The health service is identifying those who have been in contact with the patient and carrying out disinfection The regional office of Rospotrebnadzor has launched a sanitary and epidemiological investigation following reports of a possible outbreak in the Moscow Region. Experts are identifying those who have been in contact with infected individuals, organising anti-epidemic measures and issuing an order for disinfection to be carried out. The department stated that the situation is under control. There is no official confirmation of a large-scale spread of mpox between regions. Find out more новини Читати новини Новини How is mpox transmitted, and when should you see a doctor? Mpox is a viral infection transmitted primarily through close physical contact with an infected person. The World Health Organisation lists a rash, fever, headache, muscle pain, swollen lymph nodes and fatigue as possible symptoms. The infection may be more dangerous for children, pregnant women and people with weakened immune systems. The WHO recommends avoiding close contact with people who have characteristic symptoms, washing your hands and seeking medical advice if you suspect you have been infected. 已有5人入院治疗,暂无确诊病例。医生正在对他们进行感染检测,地区当局正在监测情况(N4)。

News article (English original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (English)
A monkeypox outbreak has been suspected in the Moscow region: 5 people hospitalized - Українські Національні Новини (УНН)
News Brief
A monkeypox outbreak has been suspected in the Moscow region, with five individuals hospitalized after contact with a patient suspected of having the virus. The Moscow Times reports this through UNN, and the Ministry of Health in the Moscow Region stated that all hospitalized individuals have undergone necessary examinations and had samples taken. However, there is currently no confirmed case of infection, and the patients are described as having no symptoms with satisfactory conditions. This incident follows previous cases of monkeypox in the region, including a confirmed diagnosis in February 2026. The situation remains under investigation, with no definitive information on the spread or risk level at this time.

been suspected in the Moscow region: 5 people hospitalized Kyiv • UNN October 15 2026, 08:24 PM • 3566 views Five people were hospitalized after contact with a patient suspected of having monkeypox. They have no symptoms, and their tests are still being checked. In Russia’s Moscow Region, 5 people who had contact with a patient suspected of having monkeypox were hospitalized. Doctors are testing them for the infection, while regional authorities say they are monitoring the situation. The Moscow Times reports, according to UNN. Details The Moscow Region Ministry of Health said that all those hospitalized had undergone the necessary examinations and had samples taken. They are currently under doctors’ supervision. According to preliminary data, none of the 5 patients has symptoms of the disease. Doctors describe their condition as satisfactory. At the same time, there is currently no information confirming a new case of infection. Cases of monkeypox have already been recorded in the region In February 2026, several cases of infection were detected in the Moscow suburbs. At that time, a man suspected of having monkeypox was admitted to a hospital in Domodedovo, and the diagnosis was later confirmed. A person who had been in contact with him was hospitalized afterward. Another 2 weeks later, the disease was diagnosed in 2 tourists who had arrived in St. Petersburg from Thailand. For reference Monkeypox is a viral infection that can cause fever, enlarged lymph nodes, and painful skin rashes. The disease is most widespread in African countries. According to Russian virologist Vitaly Zverev, some species of rodents are natural carriers of the virus. People can become infected through contact with infected animals, including through bites or scratches. 卫生部报告 猴痘 病例略有增加 - Algoa FM

Health Department reports slight increase in mpox cases - Algoa FM
新闻简报南非卫生部报告 2026 年 10 月 19 日 猴痘 病例略有增加。该报告于 2026 年 10 月 18 日发布,提到东开普地区是 猴痘 的所在地,但没有具体说明受影响的确切人口。该事件被确认为 猴痘 病例增加,但没有提供病例、死亡或暴露的具体数字。报告中没有明确指出健康风险。响应状态表明卫生部门已报告病例增加,但没有有关调查、测试或采取的响应措施的信息。该情况仍在调查中,报告没有提供有关 暴发 规模或与之相关的风险级别的详细信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
卫生部于 2026 年 10 月 19 日报告 猴痘 病例略有增加,事件日期为 2026 年 10 月 19 日。
地点与对象
该报告提到了南非,特别是东开普地区的 猴痘 病例,但没有具体说明受影响的确切人口。
事件
卫生部报告 猴痘 病例略有增加,这是根据官方报告确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
报告显示 猴痘 病例略有增加,但没有提供病例、死亡或暴露的具体数字。没有明确说明健康风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生部已报告了病例增加的情况,但没有有关调查、测试或采取的应对措施的信息。目前情况仍在调查中。

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

旺角桑拿顾客须接受猴痘测试 - 香港电台新闻网

News article (English original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 14 日,香港卫生防护中心在确认一名 39 岁男子感染该病毒后,敦促旺角胡同桑拿的顾客如果出现症状,应接受 猴痘 检测。据报道,该男子与俱乐部的其他男性顾客有高风险的性接触。香港当局今年已确认 31 起 猴痘 案件,其中自 8 月以来已有 22 起,其中大多数与约会应用程序的使用有关。最新的病例被认为是孤立的,而不是 暴发。作为预防措施,该俱乐部暂时关闭,接触者追踪仍在进行中,但有些患者无法追踪到他们的性伴侣。正在安排对有症状的人进行检测,建议拨打热线2125-2373进行健康评估。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年10月14日香港时间11:55。
地点与对象
香港旺角;胡同俱乐部的顾客、性接触高危人群以及 9 月 14 日或之后去过桑拿的人。
事件
一名39岁男子在旺角胡同俱乐部发生高风险性接触后确诊感染猴痘。卫生防护中心敦促俱乐部的顾客如果出现症状,请接受 猴痘 检测。
规模、影响与风险
香港今年有31宗确诊的猴痘病例,自8月以来已录得22宗。大多数案件涉及个人使用约会应用程序寻找性伴侣。最新的感染被视为孤立病例,而不是 暴发。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生防护中心建议暂时关闭胡同俱乐部,以防万一。接触者追踪正在进行中,但一些患者无法联系到他们的性伴侣。正在为有症状的人安排检测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (English original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (English)
Mong Kok sauna patrons urged to get Mpox tests - 香港電台新聞網
News Brief
On October 14, 2026, the Centre for Health Protection in Hong Kong urged patrons of the Hu-Tong sauna in Mong Kok to get Mpox tests if they exhibit symptoms, following the confirmation of a 39-year-old man with the virus. The man reportedly had high-risk sexual contact with other male patrons at the club. Authorities have confirmed 31 Mpox cases in Hong Kong this year, with 22 cases since August, and the majority linked to dating app usage. The latest case is considered isolated, not an outbreak. The club was temporarily closed as a precaution, and contact tracing is ongoing, though some patients could not trace their sexual partners. Testing is being arranged for those with symptoms, and the hotline 2125-2373 is recommended for health assessments.
Original source text (English)
Apps A A A 繁 简 Eng Menu News Homepage Latest News Video Gallery News Programmes Photo Gallery News Bulletins News Archive Send To RTHK News Error News Archive Live Video News 2026.10.19 Monday 27°C 80% Search 20252026 123456789101112 12345678910111213141516171819202122232425262728293031 Latest News Local Greater China World News Finance Sport News Bulletins Photo Gallery Video Gallery Can search within past 12 months Home Latest News Local Mong Kok sauna patrons urged to get Mpox tests 2026-10-14 HKT 11:55 Share this story The Centre for Health Protection's Albert Au says patrons of the Hu-Tong club in Mong Kok should call a hotline where Mpox screening can be arranged for anyone with symptoms. Photo: RTHK The Centre for Health Protection is urging people who have recently visited a Mong Kok sauna to get screened for Mpox, after a patron was confirmed with the viral illness. The 39-year-old man was said to have had high-risk sexual contact with other male patrons of the Hu-Tong club. Authorities have confirmed 31 Mpox cases in Hong Kong so far this year. Speaking on an RTHK radio programme on Wednesday, the head of the centre's communicable disease branch, Albert Au, said he believed the latest infection might only be an isolated case and not an outbreak. “This patient frequented a sauna named Hu-Tong at Shanghai Street in Mong Kok multiple times during the incubation period and had high-risk sexual contact there," he said. "We believe it is highly likely that he had been infected at Hu-Tong. "Since we cannot rule out the possibility that he had been infected there, we have recommended that Hu-Tong be temporarily closed as a precaution.” While the incubation period for the virus usually lasts for six to 13 days, Au said it can be as short as a day or as long as 21 days. He noted that early symptoms are similar to other viral infections, which may include fever, chills, fatigue and swollen lymph nodes. But a rash is the most crucial symptom, Au said, eventually forming into scabs. He urged those who have been to the sauna on or after September 14 to reach out to the Mpox hotline on 2125-2373 as soon as possible to undergo a health assessment. The centre will arrange testing for those with symptoms. Au said 22 confirmed cases had been recorded since August and that the majority of them had used dating apps to look for sexual partners. He said it has been a challenge for authorities to trace all contacts, as some of the infected patients had been unable to get back in touch with their sexual partners. Time
2026-10-14 HKT 11:55.
Location and population
Mong Kok, Hong Kong; patrons of the Hu-Tong club, individuals with high-risk sexual contact, and those who visited the sauna on or after September 14.
Event
A 39-year-old man was confirmed with Mpox after having high-risk sexual contact at the Hu-Tong club in Mong Kok. The Centre for Health Protection is urging patrons of the club to get Mpox tests if they have symptoms.
Scale, impact and risk
31 confirmed Mpox cases in Hong Kong this year, with 22 cases recorded since August. The majority of cases involved individuals using dating apps for sexual partners. The latest infection is considered an isolated case, not an outbreak.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Centre for Health Protection has recommended the temporary closure of the Hu-Tong club as a precaution. Contact tracing is ongoing, but some patients have been unable to reach out to their sexual partners. Testing is being arranged for those with symptoms.
